I'm really only here because my friends and an urgent care doctor made a huge deal out of it, but it feels like they're totally overreacting.\n\nDoctor: Do you have a strong fear of gaining weight, or do you feel your body size or shape is too large even when others say you are underweight?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I get this intense panic that if I eat something heavy like a burger or pizza, it'll stick to me and make me look softer or heavier overnight. When I look in the mirror, I don't see what everyone else sees—I just zero in on parts of my stomach or thighs that aren't razor-thin and feel like I've failed to be disciplined.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever make yourself vomit, use laxatives or diet pills, or exercise excessively to avoid gaining weight?\n\nPatient: No, I don't do any of that—I've never made myself throw up, taken laxatives, or used diet pills. I just manage my weight by skipping meals and keeping my eating to a minimum.\n\nDoctor: Have your periods stopped or become very irregular since your weight loss?\n\nPatient: Actually, I'm a man, so I don't get periods. That's not something that applies to me.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any physical problems from being underweight, such as feeling faint, getting dizzy, feeling cold all the time, or your heart beating unusually slowly?\n\nPatient: I guess I've been getting a bit shaky on the ladder lately and feeling completely exhausted halfway through the day, which has forced me to cut some mural jobs short. It's really not some major medical issue like everyone thinks.\n\nDoctor: About how much weight have you lost, and over what time period? Was it a significant drop, such as more than 10% of your body weight in the last 6 months?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure of the exact numbers or if it's been a ten percent drop, since I don't keep track of the scale. I just know that my clothes are hanging off of me now and my belt is on its very last hole. I couldn't tell you the exact timeframe or how much weight it's actually been.\n\nDoctor: When you think about your weight, do you believe you are too thin and want to gain weight, or do you still feel you need to lose more weight even though others say you are underweight?\n\nPatient: I definitely don't want to gain weight, and I think everyone is overreacting by calling me too thin. Honestly, I have too little energy to even think about exercising lately. I just manage my weight by skipping meals and keeping my eating to a minimum.\n\nDoctor: Have your periods stopped or become very irregular since your weight loss?\n\nPatient: Actually, I'm a man, so I don't get periods. That's not something that applies to me.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any physical problems from being underweight, such as feeling faint, getting dizzy, feeling cold all the time, or your heart beating unusually slowly?\n\nPatient: I guess I've been getting a bit shaky on the ladder lately and feeling completely exhausted halfway through the day, which has forced me to cut some mural jobs short. But honestly, I think it's just from being overworked and tired from the long hours. It's really not some major medical issue like everyone thinks.\n\nDoctor: About how much weight have you lost, and over what time period? Was it a significant drop, such as more than 10% of your body weight in the last 6 months?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure of the exact numbers or if it's been a ten percent drop, since I don't keep track of the scale. I just know that my clothes are hanging off of me now and my belt is on its very last hole. I couldn't tell you the exact timeframe or how much weight it's actually been.\n\nDoctor: When you think about your weight, do you believe you are too thin and want to gain weight, or do you still feel you need to lose more weight even though others say you are underweight?\n\nPatient: I definitely don't want to gain weight, and I think everyone is overreacting by calling me too thin. Honestly, I still catch myself thinking I could drop a few more pounds to be truly lean. When I look in the mirror, I just see parts of my stomach or thighs that don't look razor-thin yet.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your low weight is a problem and want to gain weight, or do you still feel you need to lose more weight even though other people say you are too thin?\n\nPatient: I absolutely don't think my low weight is a problem, and I feel like everyone else is completely overreacting about it. I have no desire to gain weight; in fact, when I look in the mirror and see my stomach or thighs, I still think I could drop a few more pounds to look truly lean.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel guilty or ashamed after eating, or do you intentionally keep your eating very restricted mainly because you want to stay thin and are afraid of gaining weight?\n\nPatient: Yes, if I eat a normal or heavy meal, I get hit with this immediate panic that I'll wake up looking softer or heavier the next day. Keeping my eating restricted actually feels like a source of pride and discipline, and if I fail to maintain that control, I feel like a complete failure.\n\nDoctor: When you restrict food or lose weight, is it mainly because you want to stay thin and are afraid of gaining weight, or is it more because food doesn’t seem appealing or you just forget to eat?"
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
While I tell myself and others that I'm just too busy with work or trying to save money, the truth is there is a deep fear of gaining weight. The idea of eating heavier foods like pizza or burgers panics me because I worry it will stick to me overnight and make me look soft. Keeping my weight low feels like a test of discipline, and I feel a real sense of pride in staying lean and constantly checking myself to make sure I look razor-thin.
